Report · page 36

Rosenheim Plate still in Belgrave hands

Track & Field - Rosenheim League

Rosenheim Plate still in Belgrave hands

Our mid-week Rosenheim League success story continued in 1998 as for the fifth consecutive time Belgrave's name was added to the two splendid Rosenheim Plates. Although the Eastern League Division and Final results were as emphatic as ever, just once or twice it looked as if we might get pipped for a match win as first Sutton & District got within 4 match points of the Bels. and then Hercules Wimbledon got almost as close with a 5 point deficit and then 4 points for match six as they made a fighting attempt to get into the final. Their tally for the whole season was higher than Croydon's but they just missed out by a single league point. Sutton & District were successful, however, and it was good to see their delight at having made it.

We held our home, opening, match at Tooting in the vain hope that a few extra officials from other clubs might be encouraged to show up through not having to travel to Battersea — but it didn't work out that way and the triple jump was cancelled as a result of there being no judges. As usual we were strong in the sprints, not quite so strong in the middle-distance (no-one for the 1500m) and solid on the field. For match two it was a similar story — this time an empty 800m berth and no high hurdler — but we still hung on for the win.

Match three: Croydon is always the most unpopular venue due to the fact that the meeting invariably starts late thanks to an over-running schools event. This year, however, the evening was enlivened by a super race from Ray Foley in the 3000 metres where he decimated his previous best by sticking to Herne Hill's (and Blackheath's) Dave Taylor like a limpet until with 600 metres remaining he just had to let go. Also a fine league record came from Lee Newman in the discus as he added some four metres to the previous best. But again we were short of an 800 metre man and, unusually, a pole vaulter.

Back to Tooting as the second half of the league was entered. This time we looked a lot stronger but were hit early on in the meeting when the normally so reliable Richard David found himself ruled out of the 100 metres by two false starts. M50 Alan Painter came to our rescue by stepping into the scoring 800 metre race, thereby giving us our first full team of the series.

There was fun at Sutton when the home club were unable to procure the services of a starter. Someone duly stepped in with whistle and handkerchief but of course, what with the dodgy starting and the gale at the athletes' backs, the sprint times were just a little bit suspect! A cagey victory came from Paul Coughlan in the 3 kms. where he studiously avoided doing too much work in the front rank with that ferocious wind down the back straight — and then let rip over the final 200 metres.

And so to match six. We were already assured of being Eastern Division winners but still turned out a strong team that was boosted by a superb high jump competition. The league record of 2.00 metres had been shared by four athletes — and that became six as both Richard Aspden and Samson Oni went clear at their first attempts. Maximum points for us but it was far from over as both men also took 2.05 first time and then tackled 2.10. Non-scorer Samson came so close but had to give best to Richard at this height who then went on to unsuccessfully attempt 2.15.

Ray Foley celebrated by waving to the onlookers as the announcer broadcast the fact that it was his birthday — and then helped himself to the present of six match points as he led home a Belgrave 1, 2, 3 in the 3000m.

The final — and the stakes were increased. A nervous Team Manager left her squad in charge of John Jeffery, Ray Foley and the young sprinters as she went off to watch the European Championships in Hungary. She needn't have worried; a 'phone call from Budapest's Népstadion back home to London revealed that the Plate was still in Belgrave hands. Lee Newman was the star of the evening as he launched a massive discus series of — 55.93, 56.45, 54.55, 56.50, 54.66, x — to twice break his own Rosenheim Record.

Marlon Dickson ended the Rosenheim season as he had started, by winning the 100 metres, but here we must pay credit to all the sprinters — Richard David, James Hilston, Dotun Akinbogun, Joe Panton, Michael Peacock, Nick Molloy and others. They have been a real driving force behind the Rosenheim win streak and in the last 4 x200 of the summer they turned on a spirited display to gain revenge over Herne Hill for match 6 and clock their fastest time of the season to boot.

Report · page 37

Mo's Vets make the final

Southern Counties Veterans League - Track & Field

Mo's Vets make the final

Well done Team Manager Maureen Jones. 1998 saw her take Belgrave's female veterans to their best ever season as they twice beat Serpentine to go into the Southern Counties Final. Their male colleagues, with Terry Lawton at the helm, were beset by the same problem that hits us from British League down to Young Athletes' League — a lack of middle-distance runners.

The scoring became complicated enough the previous season when, instead of a straightforward two per event in the under 50 events and one per event in the over 50 on the track, and just the one in all events on the field, it became permissable to enter an over 60 in place of an over 50. Over 60s were then scored separately in their own events. This meant that if a team had an over 60 and no other team did, then the over 60 automatically gained maximum points even if they could hardly put one foot in front of another. This rule change seemed to go through without any discussion with the clubs in the league and quite obviously helped one particular club.

This season it was revealed that points were to be awarded on an age-graded basis — virtually impossible to calculate on the night. Luckily all but one club in our Mid-London Division refused to have anything to do with this although when it came to the final, clubs had to elect to go into one of two finals — an age-graded final or a non-age-graded final.

There is now a rumour that next summer, men's and women's teams must be scored together. This will virtually wipe out all the team's chances in our division except Serpentine. Hercules Wimbledon, Herne Hill, Met. Police have almost no veteran women, Metros have mostly women, while Belgrave — well, Maureen had welcomed single competitors from other clubs into the Belgrave women's team to augment our own six or seven veteran ladies in an effort to combat the Serps. Now they will be ineligible as they will have to score for their first claim clubs even though there might be just the one or two of them — so we will be considerably weaker ourselves.

Perhaps, if this veteran competition is really required, we should look into organising our own Mid or South London competion on normal lines and not worry about the Southern Counties V.A.C. events.

Report · page 40

Lifetime Shot Putt best for veteran Mike Small

Veteran Mike Small's "summer season" for 1998 doesn't seem to have stopped yet!

In October he was still turning in hammer throws in the 45-46 metre range and then from November through to January it was time to attack the throws multi-events.

During the Tooting Throws Triathlon it all came together when he launched the hammer out to an M40 Club Record and his best throw since 1988 when he was a mere stripling in his mid-thirties! Three throws in his series beat his previous season's best and next summer he will be looking for 50 metres – which would be a lifetime best. The full series: 44.41, 48.30, 47.64, x, 45.50, 46.78. Mike's score in the throws pentathlon held in conjunction was 2163 – another personal best and a meeting record.

A further best ever performance came in a throws pentathlon at Deangate in December. The 35lb hammer gave him the chance to record his best ever distance of 13.65 and his overall score of 3294pts was another pb and second on the all time list, just behind the British Record.

January - and Mike improved yet again, creeping ever nearer to that M40 British Record. This time he notched up 3,380 points with a heavy hammer throw of 14.11.

Latest news is of a lifetime best, indoors or out, while competing in the Crystal Palace Indoor Open Meeting in February. His 13.64 was just 12 centimetres shy of the M45 British Record - and Mike turns 45 this coming March.

Mike SmallJeremy Hemming

Report · page 41

Under 20s near the very top

The 1998 season saw the work of six or seven years at last come to fruition. A small band of officials have been supporting this team since the days when Leo and Jon Coy ran the team. Then we were in the lower divisions of the South Thames area — and we gained promotion. Next season it was promotion again and every single year between then and now we have not failed to win our division and go up again. Team Managers have changed (currently John Powell is in charge) and the athletes have certainly changed. A good many of the lads that have competed for us in the National Junior League have gone on to represent the Bels. in Southern and British League — Marlon Dickson, Steve Scott, Richard David — but an equal number of talented young men have also fallen by the wayside — Rob Milner, Gavin Mason, Scott Thompson to mention just a very few. But they have all contributed to our reaching the Southern Premier Division in 1998 — where we won yet again.

As a result of our high showing the team earned the right to compete in the National Final at Cannock where we placed an outstanding 3rd among the very best in the country. Many of the team still have one or two more years as Under-20s so there could be more to come.

Report · page 42

Young Bels. hang on in top division

All credit to John Dawson for taking over the Young Athletes' team at the beginning of 1998, hardly knowing what he was letting himself in for. It was a time of some turmoil in the management of our young male teams and it showed in match one where we not only got hammered in our home meeting, we also had to endure the acid comments of officials and team managers when just about everything that could go wrong, did go wrong!

A much stronger team went to Croydon for match two but in spite of some brilliant individual results it was another last place for the team and relegation from the Southern Premier Division was beginning to look a certainty. The drama was not confined to the field of competition. An ambulance had to be called and the meeting held up while it drove down the 100 metre stretch to collect a young lad who had collapsed after his race. He was later pronounced to be fine — and yes it was a Belgrave boy. Then there was the innocuous attempt by one of our lads to query one of the results with the recorders—resulting in a completely "over the top" announcer broadcasting that a Belgrave lad was attacking one of his recorders! Who'd be a young athlete's team manager?

The first week-end in June took us up to Enfield and here the tide imperceptibly changed. Still desperately short of under 13 boys (we only had one!) we nevertheless got out of the basement for the first time with third team place.

Now faced with a trip to Norwich some hard decisions had to be taken. Relegation was still looking likely and morale was low. Should we pull out now and save the cost of further coach travel? It would look bad but at this stage did we care? Thankfully the right decision was made and 40-50 boys set out at sparrow's chirp for Norfolk. The lads really got stuck into the opposition and the result was a much more satisfactory second place. If we could just duplicate that result in the final match we would stay up.

And so to Bracknell. You wouldn't believe that it was the same team that had suffered such a devastating defeat at Battersea three months earlier. Crowds of claret and gold clad youngsters sat on the sidelines waiting to compete. Race after race was dominated by young Belgravians and the final result was a resounding first! We live on in the Southern Premier to fight another day

Notice · page 42

John Lambert to join John Dawson at the helm

Already plans are being laid for the 1999 season. John Lambert will be joining John Dawson to share the load of managing the team and with Reg Hopkins gathering some under 13s around him to tackle Sportshall athletics during the winter, we should at least start off with some lads in this age group come next summer.

We still need lots of help with the officiating because for every official we are short at any match, we lose points. We need to take a track judge, timekeeper, and a team of field event judges (two to four bodies) to every meeting. If you are going to the meeting anyway why not consider becoming part of the team by being an official. It doesn't matter if you've never done it before because we have experienced officials on hand to show you what to do.

John LambertJohn DawsonReg Hopkins

Report · page 43

Surrey County AA Sportshall Athletics

WESTCROFT LEISURE CENTRE, 8 NOV 1998

In our first outing in Sportshall Athletics since Leo Coy was the man in charge we didn't do at all badly with a team of just six boys. Team Manager Reg Hopkins must have been very pleased.

Events were two, four and six lap individual races, long jump, vertical jump and shot, obstacle race, paarlauf and 4 x 2 laps. Note that "a lap" means starting in the middle of the hall and racing to the wall – up the reverser board and back the full length of the hall – up the reverser board at the other end and back across the middle.

Terry Tsai, who showed much promise as a sprinter in the McDonalds League last summer, was fastest of the 22 competitors in the 6 lap race and Belgrave won the paarlauf and 4 x 2 lap relay.

Well done to all who took part – Barnaby and Toby Hodgeson, Terry Tsai, Hugo Sells, Roy Collier and Phillip Doorgachurn. As a team they placed 8th of the 14 clubs who took part with 317 points. Herne Hill were match winners with 427.

Under 13 Boys 2 laps: T.Tsai 24.9; B.Hodgeson 26.5. 4 laps: T.Hodgeson 59.9; H.Sells 69.4. 6 laps: T.Tsai 1:30.8; P.Doorgachurn 1:31.8; R.Collier 1:43.8. Vertical Jump: 28cm. SP: R.Collier 6.31; T.Hodgeson 5.83. Paarlauf: 1:50.7. 4x2 laps 1:42.4. Obstacle race: 2:22.5.

SPECTRUM LEISURE CENTRE, GUILDFORD, 10 JAN 1998

Several of our team were unavailable due to sickness and then Hugo went and injured his knee in the high jump leaving us with ony three people to run in the relays. Luckily he was not badly hurt. Philip Doorgachurn has been doing well in Surrey League cross country races and he made good use of his fitness by racing to the fastest time of the afternoon — out of some 30-odd competitors — in the 6 lap race.

Feature · page 45

Performance Ratings & Comparisons One mile to Marathon

Rex Bale

— Rex Bale writes —

Have you tried to compare your times for a variety of distances to see which is the best? There have been many published tables to help you to do this but a more flexible system is possible using the chart on this page. It also allows you to give yourself a performance score so that you can set targets, or perhaps in the case of veterans to stay as long as possible above a chosen points score.

The steps to follow are as follows:

1) Divide your race time by the race distance to give a rate per mile to the nearest 10 seconds. 2) Pick out this rate per mile on the left hand vertical axis and follow the horizontal line to the intersection with the vertical line for the race distance. 3) Follow the diagonal line upwards to the right hand axis and read off the points score.

Example: Running 10 miles in 61:40 (6:10 minute miles) gives a score of 1,770 points — the solid line on the chart. You will see that the points lie within a fairly narrow band — for example for the mile race, a four-minute mile scores 1,950 points whereas running it in only six-and-a-half minutes scores 1,570 points.

You could use an age graded table, again published in running magazines from time to time, to adjust for age. Just multiply the rate per mile by the factor for your 5-year age group (0.88 for M50) and follow the steps above.

This system works best for fairly flat road courses and track, not cross country where the terrain is less predictable. It is also not so reliable for marathons since as many of you will know only too well, anything can happen, and hitting "the Wall" is not accounted for in this method. Apart from this, I have personally found it very reliable and you will probably be surprised to find that your performance scores are very consistent over a wide range of race distances.

This scheme was devised by Tom Osler in his 'Serious Running Handbook', one of a plethora of books to come out of the running boom in the late 1970s and early 1980s, mostly from the USA.

Rex BaleTom Osler
See it on the printed page →
Feature · page 45

Life Membership for John Davies

Gordon Biscoe

— Gordon Biscoe writes —

Many will know of John and his background but if there is anyone who sees him only as one of the Saturday afternoon crowd who complains about his aching knees and waves the bath box under your nose we can enlighten you — he does have a past — and a very significant one.

Like several of our other stars John's first sporting love was football where he played in the amateur ranks for Kingstonian, Wimbledon and Tottenham Hotspur (well we didn't say he was perfect!)

However, in 1955 he decided a change was in order and turned to athletics. In May of that year he joined Belgrave and very quickly made his mark not only in our club but in the Athletics World in general.

From 1955 to the early 60's John's track career as a middle-distance runner fell only just so very, very short of full international status, but it should be noted that at that time he was vying with some of the great names of the past — Hewson, Chataway, Brasher and Pirie to name but a few. These men all became his regular training partners and personal friends.

Space does not permit a listing of all his achievements during this time but let's just record that he represented the A.A.A. on many occasions both at home and on the Continent, particularly in Sweden, and recorded personal best times of: 1:52 for 800m and 4:10.4 for 1 mile — all on cinders!

He did not confine himself purely to 800's or miles, also becoming Club Champion at 3 miles and turning out regularly on the road and country when needed.

In 1970 with his Track career behind him, John joined the 'Saturday Crowd' at Belgrave Hall and turned his attention to road running in earnest. That means he has now been going up to the Hall on a Saturday for 28 years — or from another angle, he's probably consumed something in the order of 1,456 cups of Brian Gorman's tea and eaten an equivalent number of sausage rolls! Amazing! And in spite of this he's completed 68 marathons including the London and is still with us!

We must mention one of his other highlights, which was of course meeting Pam while training at Tooting. Pam, an International herself, must surely share in the credit for the honour of Life Membership bestowed upon John — for keeping our boy on the straight and narrow all this time!

Throughout his 28 years as one of the Saturday Crowd John has performed an invaluable service to the Club in his own quiet unassuming way: helping out wherever needed, making new members feel welcome, advising and encouraging some of our younger ones and taking them out training. Then there's that Bath Box. It was his original idea to pass this around on Saturdays after training, to help offset some of our heating costs. Over the years he has literally raised hundreds of pounds.

John DaviesGordon BiscoeHewsonChatawayBrasherPirieBrian GormanPam

Results · page 46

Second at the 'Valley'

PUMA TVH ROAD RELAY STOCKLEY PK, 28 MAR 1998 In the second running of the revived 'TVH' road relays we moved up to the silver medals from last year's bronze. A super run from Gary Staines had him equalling the course record and taking the Bels. into the lead from the springboard of Graham Adam's opening stage but there was no denying the solid Shaftesbury outfit from thereon. A half-fit Bruce Barton kept us in the medals but then an uncharacteristic below-par run from Kevin Quinn had us slipping back to 4th. We were looking for a time perhaps a minute faster from young Kev. but even had he achieved it, the final result would have been exactly the same. A strong fight back by Roger Alsop took us to the line in 2nd and an overall time 16 seconds faster than that of '97.

Results · page 46

Reebok SEAA 12-Stage Road Relay, Thurrock 5 Apr 1998

Once again we lost many of our faster men in the week preceding the race so went for the tactic of attempting to remain competitive for as long as possible by putting the faster men on early stages. It worked pretty well, for by the middle of the race the gaps had opened up and creditable performances by the lads on the latter laps gave us 15th overall and a place in the National 12-Stage – should we want it! Sadly, with the warm weather training and preparations for the oncoming track season taking precedence, we couldn't even muster twelve names for the National and had to give it a miss. For four years now we have failed to line up in Sutton Park to face the best of the rest of the country. It's a habit we need to break.

Report · page 46

National Veterans C.C. Championships, Lloyd Park, 28 Mar 1998

The National Veterans Cross Country Championships were held last March at one of our local venues, Lloyd Park in Croydon, and this provided a fitting occasion for Belgravians to gather in memory of the late Laurie O'Hara who had died almost exactly a year before.

The Club had donated a new silver salver, the Laurie O'Hara Trophy, which was presented to the winner of the M65 race by Mrs Jean O'Hara, Laurie's widow, who had kindly attended the meeting with her son and his family. The start of the M50's and 60's race was preceded by a few moments silence. The end of the silence was greeted with a touching spontaneous applause for Jean and her son Laurie by the athletes, many of whom had known and raced Laurie in similar events for over twenty five years.

Lloyd Park was bathed in warm sunshine for the event which was admirably organised by Southern Vets. The tough, undulating three lap course proved a true test for the runners, although perhaps a little over demanding for some of our less fit colleagues. It was also an excellent spectator course and it was good to see so many Belgravians who had come to run in spite of injury or lack of fitness, and others who had just come to support.

Performance of the day was by Jim Estall, second in the M40 race. The M40 team actually got 6th place helped by an excellent run from John Mather who had been told "never run again". Pat Newell had been moving through nicely to the medal positions until his hamstring went at the end of the second lap and this cost our M60's a team bronze. Frank Gander was a revelation over the tough course and at the end looked as though he could go round a couple more times.

Laurie O'HaraJean O'HaraLaurieJim EstallJohn MatherPat NewellFrank Gander

Report · page 46

Paul Evans' London Record Dented

LONDON MARATHON 26 APR 1998 Paul Evan's marvellous record of achievement in this race took a dent when he was forced to pull out at 18 miles due to stomach cramps; his halfway time was inside 65 minutes. With Gary Staines taking on pacemaking duties and therefore only going half the distance it was therefore left to our redoubtable veteran Jim Estall to lead home the Belgrave contingent.

It was good to see two Belles finishing within a few seconds of each other, just outside the top hundred. A couple more to join Nikki Haines and Rachel Weston and we might have figured in the women's team race.

Dave McMillan looked absolutely awful as he clawed his way along Upper Thames Street with a couple of miles to go. He was some twenty minutes or so outside his target but one can hardly believe that this man, who spent months in a wheelchair recuperating from terrible injuries, was there at all.

The results that follow have been gleaned from the columns of The Times. Sorry if we've missed anyone.

Results · page 47

Belgrave - Nonsuch 4 x 2.25m Relay, Nonsuch Park, 9 Jul 1998

This was the second time that Bill Law's company had sponsored this Dave Denton organized relay in our name and again the opposition from Alf Tupper ACM, Upton O Good, Epsom Oddballs and others enjoyed the chance to race our lads in claret and gold.

Gary Staines started by carrying out a demolition job on Ray Foley's lap record while Kevin Quinn trimmed his best for this circuit. In his first relay for Belgrave Paul Coughlan extended the 'A' team's lead but just to make the finish more exciting, veteran Alan Black then ambled round giving Marcello Bizio the chance to take the 'B's ahead. Gary, going for a second circuit, was originally supposed to finish off for the 'B's who should then have been disqualified as he had already run – but he and Ray were busy swapping numbers and teams in an attempt to make the finish as close as possible and give each other the chance of some stiff competition at the end. As it turned out Ray was able to operate at 15 seconds inside his old lap record pace and Gary was unable to get near him in spite of running what became the second fastest lap of all time – and the 'A' team should have been disqualified – but wasn't. Confused? The team manager certainly was.

Results · page 47

Yacht Handicap 3 miles, Wimbledon, 3 Oct 1998

The "Yacht" continues to maintain its popularity. The entry list was up slightly again and with a bit of effort we could surely push it well into the thirties once more.

Things don't get any easier for Handicapper Arthur Bruce, however. He now has to work out projected times for athletes that range from 10 year old Louise Collier to septuagenarian Charlie Manning, from "come-back" specialist Vic Butcher to flying Moroccan Najibe Hliouat. Throw in a couple of runners who missed their proper start times – Steve Fowler went off at about 14:25 instead of 14:00 while James Skilbeck-Nelson set out much later than he should have done – and poor Jacqui Smiter's asthma attack in mid-race, and one can see that there was as much drama in the event as ever.

Tania Sturton took advantage of Arthur's generosity to cut through those setting out ahead of her and reach home first with Vic Butcher in tow. Ex-long jumper Syreeta Stracey, now converting to middle-distance running, surprised by taking third place.

Report · page 48

League hopes 'blown away' in first fixture

SURREY CROSS COUNTRY LEAGUE DIVISION 1 – RACE 1 BROCKWELL PARK, 17 OCT 1998

League hopes 'blown away' in first fixture

A "hoped for" place in the top four teams proved optimistic. A closely fought battle finally saw only 104 points covering positions 3rd to 8th with the Bels. near the back of that group in 6th.

The Hounslow boys dominated the race, showing no regard for the blustery conditions. South London's Stuart Major, Hercules Wimbledon's Adrian Marriott, Herne Hill's Ian Munt and our own Najib Hliouat ran with them in the leading group which set out at such a fearful pace that Lee Hurst (30th in last year's 'National' remember), racing with a heart monitor for the first time, found himself running into trouble within the first mile.

Najibe, it seemed, had bitten off more than he could chew and although still fighting with the leaders as the first lap came to an end, pulled out of the race at half way. Lee Hurst was now recovering from the blistering start and holding on as our leading scorer with Jim Estall making a great showing close behind and staking an early claim for the position of top veteran. What a pity that Veteran International duty will rule him out of the second match and thus not allow him to retain his title.

And so to the finish. Hounslow, so strong at the front, had to go back a fair way to close in their ten men and thus let in the solid Thames Hare & Hounds team who always pack so well in the 20s to 40s. After that it was almost impossible to immediately judge the next seven teams.

Will Cockerell scored for us for the first time and Italian Saul Armanini should be well pleased with his first run in Belgrave colours – he will be eligible to score for us come the new year.

Hounslow were just as assertive at the sharp end of the youngsters' event and easily carried off both the under 17 and under 13 team races. Thanks to Reg Hopkins' persuasive powers, though, Belgrave were one of only three teams that managed to turn out a full quota of runners in the older lads' race and were repaid with third place, led home as ever by Manuel Garcia.

No club had a full five in the under 13 contest where Belgrave numbered four boys who excelled in gaining second, top scorer being Philip Doorgachurn.

'Belles' start strongly

WOMENS SURREY CC LEAGUE DIVISION 2 – RACE 1 TILFORD, 17 OCT 1998

'Belles' start strongly

17 teams closed in

After a closely fought match at Tilford our women's cross country team showed their new Team Manager Jacqui Smiter that they have no intention in spending longer than is necessary in Division Two of the Surrey League.

Report · page 48

Priory Cross Country Relays, Reigate, 7 Nov 1998

Everyone seems to want to run on the first leg and this time Paul Coughlan got the nod. South London clearly led the field home thanks to Stuart Major's opening 13:15 while Paul's 7th place among a knot of other runners gave Kevin Quinn plenty of opposition to get stuck into. Kevin moved up two places before handing over to Junior Galley who dropped back one slot during his circuit. How this man runs on the grassy slopes without spikes we'll never know.

Once again Clive Gilby was our fastest man, although not so fit as the same time last season when his 13:13 gave him 3rd fastest of the day. Clive's run gave us our highest placing throughout the race but also put an unfit Graham Adams, just off the plane from holidaying in the USA, in the unenviable position of setting out with Boxhill's Simon Fairbrother on his tail. Tony Barden finished off for the Bels. and then prepared to cheer on his partner Lisa Thompson running for the SLH women's 'B' team.

The Belgrave women's team were a revelation. Fifteenth in 1997, this time around they cut their overall time by 3 minutes to move up to 6th – "Just one place lower than the men's team" a gleeful Jacqui Smiter announced.

Tania Sturton set the ball rolling by reducing her time by 1:40 to set our fastest time of the day in coming home 8th. She had to be at her best because Debbie Hearn had taken the initiative at the start for the 'B' team and completed her lap just two places behind in 18:32 – last year she was our fastest with 19:07. Cathy Eastham maintained the pressure by holding on to 8th and taking a minute off her previous best while Syretta Stracey moved the team up another two places with an outstanding 18:21 and then looked as if she could hardly believe her performance herself.

Vicky Pincott running for her first-claim club Horsham Blue Star's 'B' team clocked 19:43.

Now Jacqui's team go top

WOMENS SURREY CC LEAGUE DIVISION 2 – RACE 2 BROCKWELL PARK, 14 NOV 1998

Now Jacqui's team go top

Report · page 49

So near to an historic double win

SOUTH OF THAMES C.C. ASSOCN. "JUNIOR" RACE BECKENHAM, 14 NOV 1998

So near to an historic double win

20 teams closed in.

8 teams closed in.

Due to the stringent barring clauses, one of which is the exclusion of all scoring members of a previous winning team, only two teams have ever taken home the winner's shield in consecutive years. Aldershot F&D did it in '73 and '74, and then again in '85 and '86. Belgrave have to go back to '45 and 46. As winners in 1997 the challenge was on. We lost two outstanding runners when Mark Miles found that he had to work and Najibe Hliouat went missing but there was still a chance that we might pull it off.

Within seconds of the start Jason Lobo stamped his mark on the race. Loping away from the field, he added 25 metres to his lead with each circuit of the first two small laps and once into the woods was out of sight of the chasing bunch, eventually coming home nearly a minute clear. As Commonwealth Games 800 metre representative he has not done much in the way of cross country for the last few years – but we certainly hope he does some more!

Paul Coughlan and Kevin Quinn made more conservative starts while Junior Galley, although much happier on tarmac, had "gone for it" in a big way. They were 9th, 20th and 24th after the opening rush and had moved up again as the Ash Plantation was tackled – 5th, 14th and 19th. Blackheath and Crawley also had plenty of men forward but at this stage the race looked ours.

A mile or so later, however, it was very much closer. Paul was now in 3rd and Kevin 9th but Junior was finding the slippery banks far from his liking and had slipped back to 30th with Tony Binda and Mike Nouch drawing ever closer to him. Crawley, with four men in 13 and another to back them up, were now edging us by a single point.

While Jason Lobo crossed the line in utmost comfort a battle royal was drawing to a close for 2nd and 3rd places. Our 'Kiwi' Coughlan and Klepacz of Portsmouth had both fallen on the last lap but as the funnel was approached it was the Belgravian who prevailed. He was "all in" but delighted to have got himself silver medal, having run out of his socks in more ways than one: he had actually left them at home and had raced without any! Maybe he should do it more often.

A super run from Kevin had got him up to 8th – but where was our 4th man? Eventually it was veteran Tony Binda, finishing like a train, who closed in our four. Mike Nouch had also got the better of Junior on that last lap and now it was time to tot up the scores.

The Crawley team manager told us that his men had 46. We had 44 and Blackheath 48. We'd done it – or had we? Something was not quite right and a recount by the Sussex men showed that their arithmetic was not quite so hot as their running – they now made it 43 and had in fact pipped us by a point.

Never mind, it was a great race, and the eight-man Brent Shield stayed in our hands thanks to Junior, who somehow kept going when he really would have preferred to go home, and tough old dogs John Mather and Don Anderson. And think of the good news – with only Coughlan and Lobo barred we have the makings of another good showing in 1999.

126 finished

Report · page 50

Second on the Common but 'Thames' increase their lead

SURREY CROSS COUNTRY LEAGUE DIVISION 1 – RACE 2 WIMBLEDON COMMON, 21 NOV 1998

Second on the Common but 'Thames' increase their lead

Our home match and things were looking a bit bleak as, with only twenty minutes to start time, we had as many non-scorers on the declaration list as scorers. And then it came together: Clive Gilby (took him longer to get there than he thought it would), Lee Hurst (with Carly having travelled from Manchester to Euston, to Waterloo, to Wimbledon), Ray Foley (with Anna and new baby Tess), Phil Carstairs (would he ever let us down) and others all arrived within a few minutes. By the time a claret and gold scarf was dropped to set the field on its way we had 14 scorers and 5 others on the line – but there were several expected Belgrave runners who never made it.

Hounslow suffered badly as their international trio gave the race a miss but the other main protagonists from race 1 all joined battle again in the fine, crisp conditions. After the dash across the golf course and down the hill to the playing fields, five runners gradually detached themselves from the main field. Through the woods and then climbing back up to the end of the first lap it was Munt (Herne Hill), Matthews (Thames H&H), Major (South London), Marriott (Hercules Wimbledon) and Lee Hurst bowling along at the front with barely a whisker between them. Then came the main stream of the pack with another three Belgravians in the first twenty. Once again it looked like a tight race between 2nd and 4th but it was obvious that Thames Hare & Hounds were tightening their grip on the league with a full team of ten white vests bearing the saltire in the first 50.

There was much chopping and changing among the leaders during lap two but the pattern had been set. Lee held on to his 5th while Clive Gilby showed a terrific improvement in form to get into the Surrey League top ten for the first time. Paul Coughlan might have been expected to have been a little jaded after his all-out effort in the South of Thames race the previous week but he and rival Ray Foley had great runs and as a result we had four home in the very thick of the action.

It was good to have Mike Kazimierski getting back to fitness again. Steve Fowler always produces the goods when he turns out and Phil Carstairs was another to turn in a strong run. Our Junior Captain Kevin Quinn reached his best ever league position and scanning through the results we note that no other under 20 appeared ahead of him. The ever-dependable Mike Webb and Will Cockerall closed in the team with Saul Armanini showing once again that he'll soon be helping reduce our points score.

Many thanks to the pointsmen who gave up their Saturday afternoon runs to assist with the race – to trail-layers Don Anderson and Tony Binda – to the timekeepers and judges and recorders – and the results team. By the time a pint of Guinness was being supped in the 'King of Denmark' where the results were being pored over by the core members of the team and their supporters, we could feel pleased with a day which had seen us place 2nd, move up to 3rd overall and promote a successful event.

Report · page 50

County Cross Country Champs. 12 Dec 1998

What a disappointing day. At Old Coulsdon for the 'Surreys' the steady drizzle soaked us all through and the liquid mud around the start area crept into trainers and kit bags alike. But the real 'downer' was the fact that barring our under 20 men, we were unable to complete a single team and, in the younger age groups, both male and female, we failed to turn out any runners at all.

We really ought to be making a lot more of an impression in the Surrey County Champs. than we do. Ok so we won the men's race a couple of years back and the women's event a while before that but so often it's a struggle to find the runners who'll do it. This year, with Jim Estall and Ray Foley running so well and with Kevin Nash expected to take part we thought we had a really good start in the men's event. Graham Adams is coming back to fitness so Roger Alsop and Junior Galley were persuaded to make up the team. But as the gun was fired we were still only five in number and when the results were published later in the week we were amazed to find that Kevin Nash had run in the Hampshire event!

Jim's run proved to be another cracker and Ray had his best ever 'County' run, even having the audacity to get up to our 'super-vet's' shoulder and overtake him for a brief period with a mile or so to go. Congratulations to team winners South London Harriers it was their first win in 23 years.

We were also one short in the senior women's team race where Helen Maskrey led home Syreeta Stracey and Cathy Eastham who were surprised to find that the distance was a full 8 kilometres. Tania Sturton and Debbie Hearn kept the Belgrave flag flying in the 4.8 kms. race in which both the under 20s and under 17s ran together.

James Venning came back from Southampton University to run in our under 20 men's team but running in the mud with spikes that were no more than 'rivets', it was not surprising that he had trouble keeping on his feet. New members Robert Palmer and Tom Knowles completed our team but we missed Kevin Quinn who was out with 'flu' and could manage no higher than 5th team.

Report · page 51

Promotion beckons for the Belles

WOMENS SURREY CC LEAGUE DIVISION 2 – RACE 3 PETERSHAM, RICHMOND PARK, 9 JAN 1999

Promotion beckons for the Belles

17 teams closed in

Another win for the Belles meant that we are vitually certain now of winning the Division Two title this winter — but it could so easily have gone wrong. With only four Belgrave girls certain to run in the days leading up to the race (and you need five for a full team), team boss Jacqui Smiter was delighted to receive a 'phone call from Nikki Haines on the morning of the race asking if she could run. Nikki works for Adidas and spends a great deal of her time abroad these days, making it difficult for her to race as often as she'd like. Even then Jacqui had to go through a warm up routine when Tania Sturton and Helen Maskrey only got to the start with five minutes to spare — the joys of being a team manager!

Conditions were cold and muddy but they seemed to suit Nikki who celebrated her return to the team by leading them home in 7th place. Helen and Tania weren't far behind and with Sam Symonds running well and Nina Mills closing in the team we'd done it, with some 20-odd points to spare. Our lead in the race for promotion was now up to 4 league points over Stragglers with Dulwich Park Runners a further point in arrears.

In the younger age groups Genya Marek ran well among the under 15s and Debbie Hearn continued to make her presence felt among the under 17s, this time with 3rd place.

In the youngest group of all it was a complete turn-around from recent years as we fielded no fewer than eight girls with the smallest lass of all, Louise Collier, spearheading the Belgrave pack.

Report · page 51

Young guns go for it!

SURREY CROSS COUNTRY LEAGUE DIVISION 1 – RACE 3 PETERSHAM, RICHMOND PARK, 16 JAN 1999

Young guns go for it!

A thrilled Adrian Marriott raised his arm aloft as he crossed the line for Hercules Wimbledon at the head of a good class field at Petersham to stake a clear lead in the individual competition. It had been a hard fought win and our own Kassa Tadesse, not as fit as he would like, had to suffer the indignity of placing outside the frame — nevertheless with our highest individual league position of the winter so far.

But even though we had no-one in the first three it was a good day for the Bels. At last we were able to see what Kevin Nash is made of — and we like it. This young man's got great talent and at not quite 22 years of age we look forward to many more outings from him in the future. Another to make his début in the team and to earn instant respect was Hassan Raidi. At just turned 20 this young Moroccan really looks the business and with his countryman Najibe Hliouat, 21, setting out a little more cautiously than he did at Brockwell Park we suddenly found that we had exciting company for stalwart Jim Estall. Jim was suffering from backache but it was going to take more than that to keep him down the field.

It was good to see Roger Alsop easing back into racing and he and Will Cockerell had a rare old ding-dong over the second lap, passing some ten or a dozen runners around the second lap as they closed in the ten.

It is difficult to see now how the team positions can be changed much in the final race. Although we pulled back 50 points on Thames Hare & Hounds, they are still over 200 clear. Once again we have paid the penalty for a poor start at Brockwell Park. Down in the basement there seems to be no way that Boxhill Racers and Hercules Wimbledon can avoid the drop.

We only had three lads out in the youngsters race — leading to the ribbing from a rival team manager after the race, "I see your Youth Development Scheme's doing well!"

Notice · page 51

A new course for our home races

The chances are that next winter we will be running over a new course on Wimbledon Common. A series of meetings have been held between the two golf Clubs that use the Common - London Scottish and Wimbledon Common - and athletic clubs: Thames Hare & Hounds, Hercules Wimbledon, Veterans A.C., Wimbledon Windmilers, Lauriston Runners and ourselves.

Cross country races have often been held without a warning being given to the golfers and as a result, golfing tournaments have been held up. Additionally there has been some concern about runners being hit by golf balls as they race out of the wooded areas and across the fairways.

For some time we have been thinking of reverting to our old course starting not far from the Pound near the corner of Cannizaro Road and Parkside and as a result of the safety concerns shown by both runners and golfers, now seems to be the time to do it.

The course will head for the Windmill across The Plain, then pass the Mounds, turn left down towards Queensmere, go alongside the lake and up hill to the Windmill, returning to the start via what we know as The Avenue and Memorial Ride. Not once will we cross a single fairway. Shorter courses based on this circuit will be suitable for very young runners.

The Common Rangers have agreed to help us with a bit of 'gardening' to smooth out the tussocks and remove some posts and as we will be using horse rides in a few places, riding will not be permitted on these stretches during advised race days.

See it on the printed page →
Report · page 52

We actually finish a team in the 'Southern'!

SEAA C. C. CHAMPS., PARLIAMENT HILL, 30 JAN 1999

At last! It makes things so much more worth while when we can round up enough people to actually complete a team — never mind where they came — at least we've now got something to work on.

After weeks of rain this was Parliament Hill Fields at its best (worst?). The initial climb up the hill was like a skating rink, the dips were full of black porridge and the ditch, well, there were runners going down like ninepins as they misjudged the downward slope and the leap across the mud.

We had three girls in action but each in different age groups so were unable to close in a female team. When you look at the results, that Junior Women's race managed to bring together just three complete teams of four runners. Would it be possible for us to find three others to join Debbie Hearn for next year and who knows, maybe pick up our first Southern medals in aeons. Good running came from Tania Sturton, currently our only under 20, as she claimed our highest place of the day with 34th. It was pleasing to see that one of our Folkestone members from a couple of seasons back — Janine Brown — got up to 25th and actually closed in the winning Ashford AC's scoring four. Cathy Eastham did not relish 5 miles of 'Parly Hill' but did well to finish half-way down the senior field.

Among the Senior Men Lee Hurst quite obviously did not enjoy the mud and at 66th was well down on his 12th of last winter. Blackheath's Mark Steinle was up and away by the end of the first circuit and seemed to float over the morass to come home half a minute clear. He was clearly the superior runner on the day but just how much the conditions affected the runners can be judged by the fact that his winning time was 4 minutes slower than last year.

Phil Carstairs often seems to turn in a good 'Southern' and this one was no exception, while Italian Saul Armanini, in his first ever scoring race for the Bels, finished much further up the field than we anticipated. Saul is going to be a very useful man to have around. Mike Webb, back from a business trip to Singapore, and Rob Harding, who had an excellent run having left his push-bike at home for a change and taken the tube from New Cross, meant that we had five home in just outside the first quarter of the field. Finally the tall figure of Jerome Curtat, a native of Metz in France, kept himself going through the slime to close in our team. With another 79 teams behind us we were pleased enough with our result.

Report · page 52

A sorry finish to the Men's Surrey League

SURREY C. C. LEAGUE DIVISION 1 – RACE 4 COULSDON, 27 FEB 1999

Just to keep our feet firmly on the ground after team wins in our two previous outings, our perfomance in the final league fixture was, well, 'a disaster' is probably too strong a description when we still ended up in the top half of the division, so let's settle for 'abysmal'.

There was nothing we could do about it really. Five runners came out during the preceding week with injuries and other commitments, two more overnight with colds and then another one having to work. Four more didn't make it on the afternoon — and then two came out after one lap of Farthing Downs. Miraculously, after losing fourteen men we still had ten scorers, thanks to race walker Mabon Dane who closed in our team.

Thames Hare & Hounds turned out their strongest squad to retain the league trophy and Herne Hill also got it together in a big way, storming through from 5th league place to 2nd, pushing us down to 3rd. … And there was us thinking that the final team positions were unlikely to change in this last event.

Hounslow's Junior international Sam Haughian was awesome as he destroyed the chances of Taylor and his club-mate Simpson — one of the best individual wins for a long time.

For the Bels., we welcomed Charles 'bandana' Herrington into the team, unfortunately only as a non-scorer. Sporting claret head-gear, Charles was always our top scorer, ahead of Najibe Hliouat (before he called it a day) and comfortably up on Paul Coughlan, who was having a good run himself. Rob Harding is getting stronger with each outing while Phil Carstairs was way below his best and suffering from a cold which really should have stopped him running.

Well, that's it for another league campaign. Our performances in the last few winters have been solid and we have established ourselves as a "top half of the league" team, but if we want to win it we are going to have to come out with all guns blazing in race one.

R.Collier, K.Taylor and P.Doorgachurn also ran but positions unknown.

Notice · page 52

The Bill Merryman Cup for the Young Belgravian of the Year

When Bill Merryman passed away he left the Club a sum of money and it was his wish that we purchase a trophy to be competed for annually. After much deliberation the Committee have decided on a handsome cup which will be awarded annually to the Young Belgravian of the Year. It will be awarded to the young man or women that the Committee decides has made the most outstanding contribution to the Club throughout the previous 12 months - a contribution that is not necessarily one that can be measured in terms of athletic excellence or the most team points.

Bill Merryman

Report · page 53

... but Belles promoted as Division 2 winners

WOMEN'S SURREY C. C. LEAGUE DIVISION 2 – RACE 4, LLOYD PARK, 27 FEB 1999

While the men were struggling at Coulsdon, the women's race at nearby Lloyd Park saw the Belles turn on their strongest performance of the winter to win the final Division Two fixture and thereby make absolutely certain of a return to Division One for the 1999/2000 season.

The win was emphatic with all five scorers closing in within a dozen places. Nikki Haines and Helen Maskrey led the way with Tania Sturton beathing down their necks and carrying off the prize for the best Under 20 throughout the overall competition. Both Syreeta Stracey and Cathy Eastham have come on tremendously throughout the season there is no doubt that we now have a nucleus of runners who should be able to maintain a Division One place. We just need a few more runners to commit themselves to racing and we'll then have the strength in depth that is so necessary to maintain upward progression.

Under 13 N.Richardson ran but position unknown

Report · page 54

Harriers swoop on Sparrow's Den

Dewar Shield & Coleman Cup Retained in 100th Championship Race

SOUTH OF THAMES C.C. CHAMPIONSHIP SPARROW'S DEN, WEST WICKHAM, 13 FEB 1999

Cross country days don't come much sweeter than this. Hard work during the preceding ten days resulted in thirteen men packing into their starting pen at Sparrow's Den, West Wickham, spilling over into the next pen as a friendly team without aspirations as to the team prizes offered to line up behind us.

Just to remind you, the team that wins the Dewar Shield for the six-a-side championship race gets its scoring men barred from all subsequent races for the next ten years — and as we won it last year, we did not expect to be able to pull it off again. Therefore all effort was put behind the twelve-a-side race for the Coleman Cup. Various members of our team had other ideas though and in spite of the fact that this 100th running of the event was being held on Blackheath's patch and they seemed to have the strongest outfit, claret and gold was prominent among the leading colours streaming around the first circuit of the playing fields. It was Herne Hill who seemed to have the most men forward while Chris Knight of GEC Avionics took an early lead, chased hard by our own Najibe Hliouat. Up through the woods and then down again and it could be seen that Blackheath had three or four in the top ten and Portsmouth were also packing well.

Halfway through the race and Najibe was becoming isolated as Knight drew ever further away to become a convincing winner. Paul Coughlan was chasing a whole mob of runners fighting for top ten positions and Roger Alsop was running way above his early winter form; he had to, because behind him four more Belgravians were disputing who was going to get into the scoring six. It was almost impossible to tally the scores of the four main teams but it did seem that we were just shading Blackheath for the championship while Herne Hill were slipping back a little. It was those Portsmouth vests that were difficult to pick up though and several onlookers believed that they might be the men in charge.

As expected, the twelve man event looked more of a certainty, with Mike Kazimierski, Charlie Dickinson and Junior Captain Kevin Quinn hovering around the 50s and Rob Harding and Don Anderson closing in the dozen. Kevin had no intention of getting into the scoring six — he is still planning an assault on the Junior race next winter and doesn't want to get barred — but Chas. Dickinson did not appreciate him running a faartlek session and getting under his feet, eventually snapping with a "Sod off and get up the field will you!"

The final circuit saw Will Cockerell come charging through, displacing Saul Armanini as sixth man and all but getting the best of Phil Carstairs as well. Both were breathing down the neck of Captain 'Gerry' Adams who in turn had his sights on Roger. Paul Coughlan's recent heavy mileage left him unable to jump the bunch ahead of him which included several black vests of the home club. Meanwhile, towards the front Najibe had been caught by Thames's Oxborough and then by Dover of Herne Hill, but he was hanging on grimly to his 4th place.

A good crowd of Belgrave spectators including former Team Managers Bill Lucas and Gordon Biscoe were desperately urging on their men but it was going to have to be left to the man who currently does the pencil and paper work to tot up the numbers on the discs. While Team Manager Alan Mead came up with a Belgrave score of 95, John Jeffery was looking over the shoulder Blackheath's John Baldwin — and then came scuttling back with, "They've got 103." The Portsmouth man was bemoaning the fact that one of his forward placed runners had dropped out of the race with stitch, and they would have won it otherwise. News hound Martin Duff picked up on this in his AW report — but isn't having sound wind and fitness all part of the race?

Our boys were cock-a-hoop and as our President rounded them up for a team photo' we now contemplated the fact that we have another six men barred from the race. Surely we'll be way down the field next year.

Blackheath Harriers had laid on a fine celebratory buffet at their club house for this 100th race. Past winners were in attendance, including our own Denis Brickwood, Bill Lucas, Charlie Walker and Roger Alsop. Fine words were spoken and toasts drunk — it was good old traditional harrier evening. With Derek Crookes, in his 23rd year as the Association's Secretary, keeping the proceedings moving, Hon. Treasurer Jim Heathfield and Committee man Alan Black also there, together with Past President Gordon Biscoe, we had as strong a Belgrave presence as there had been among the runners during the afternoon. … And when Marianne Black, Pat Mead and Jim Heathfield picked up three of the four raffle prizes our day was complete.

180 finished.

These photo's from the lens of Jeremy Hemming who will be pleased to supply copies at £1 each. Contact the Editor of this magazine if you want one.

Feature · page 54

Three in a row?

We now have to go back to the post war years of 1945-51 to find a sequence of South of Thames team results that match what we have achieved in the last couple of winters. And remember, we only lost the Junior race before Christmas by one point!

Surely we can't win the Championship next year and make it three in a row — or can we?

The race will be held at Crawley on 22nd January 2000 and the distance will be reduced to 10 kms due to the fact that it will be held the week before the Southern CC Champs.

Just look at the men who are still eligible, not to mention those who didn't get into our scoring six this time around:

Spencer Barden James Ellis-Smith Paul Evans Lee Hurst Jason Lobo Mark Miles Kevin Nash Hassan Raidi Matt Yates

Report · page 55

Mean Fiddler!

Derek Manning comes up trumps again

No, we're not casting aspersions on Derek's abilities as Hon. Treasurer. It's just that we must congratulate him on yet another triumph on the occasion of Burns Night 1999 when, with Lionel Mann masterminding the culinary side of things, we had the best 'do' at Belgrave Hall in years. All previous Burns Nights have been great occasions but this one exceeded them all: the Scotch, the Haggis, the Piper, the folk duo, the Ceilidh, Derek's performance as ventriloquist with brother Charlie as the dummy - but most of all that fiddler. Can't wait for next year

Derek ManningLionel MannCharlie

3 Wimbledon, VAC 4.8 miles CC: 4, M.Nouch 26:46. 4 Newcastle, Great North Run Half Marathon: It seems as if this race has finally bitten off more than it can chew. There were so many runners that those in the bulk of the the field were reduced to long periods of walking or even standing still on the totally clogged road to South Shields. Up front Kassa Tadesse got stuck into Messrs. Thugwane and Mutai but once again it was Paul Evans proving to be the fastest 'home' runner as he just got the best of Blackheath's Steinle. Jim Estall didn't seem particularly pleased with his run but as first veteran home in a faster time than last year, and in worse conditions, he's being a little hard on himself. 1, J.Thugwane (RSA) 1:02:32; 2, J.Mutai (Bromsgrove & Redditch) 1:02:50; 3, M.Fiz (ESP) 1:03:30; ... 5, P.Evans 1:04:12; 7, K.Tadesse 1:04:41; 37, J.Estall 1:09:48. 4 Kingston Running Festival. Records · page 60

Ranking Lists 1998 - Men's Track & Field

Compiled by Alan Mead

Compiled by Alan Mead, [address withheld]. Telephone: [phone number withheld] Email: [e-mail withheld] Errors, omissions and updates welcomed. Acknowledgements to Bob Bridges, John Dawson, Cynthia James, John Jeffery, Derek Jones, Bill Laws, Terry Lawton, Lionel Mann, Charlie Manning, Peter Matthews (NUTS), Pete Mulholland, John Powell and Jacqui Smiter for providing some of the results and team compositions.

A = Altitude. d = Recorded in decathlon. dq = Disqualified u = Unconfirmed w = Wind assisted, or obviously wind assisted and no wind reading taken. A blank in the wind reading column indicates that the wind reading is not known or was not taken but there is no particular reason to believe that the performance was wind assisted. § = Whistle start. † = Rolling start. Dwain Chamber's reaction time of 0.063 at Johannesburg was faster than the permitted 0.10 and although not classed as a false start at the time, has been anotated as a rolling start by the National Union of Track Statisticians.

· Where wind readings are not known or where there is not an obviously superior performance by an individual among an assortment of manual, electric or windy times or distances then each performance is shown. · Figures in brackets after the athlete's name indicate their World and National Ranking. E.g. (=17-1) indicates equal 17th in the World and 1st in the UK. Where two or more figures are shown separated by / then the last figure is for their age group and the preceding figures are for age groups above. E.g. (27/4) for a U20 indicates 27th best among Seniors and 4th best among U20.

Notice · page 66

Javelin Changes

Watch out for changes in the women's javelin throw this coming summer. A new specification is being introduced and although the weight will remain the same, the centre of gravity of the new implement will be moved forward by 3cm thus, hopefully, reducing the chances of 'flat' throws. The change will make a difference to the distances thrown and as a result, old records will be disregarded and a new World Record (the longest throw of 1999) will be accepted as the new mark on 1st January 2000. So it's goodbye to Petra Felke's 80.00m throw from 1988. It is unlikely that all tracks will be able to restock immediately with the new style 600gm javelin so the best we can do is attempt to record on the result sheets which style of javelin was used.

Petra Felke

Notice · page 66

Win Shepherd retires from officiating

It was the end of an era when the last Thameside League match of 1998 came to a conclusion. Win Shepherd, for so many years the lady (along with Jean) calculating the results, had decided that she would be retiring from officiating.

Win, our first ever lady President, has been a part of our Women's Track & Field scene for so long that it is going to be difficult to believe that she's not there when the 1999 season gets under way. Hopefully she will get along to watch one or two meetings, but in the meantime, Win, we wish you well and thank you for the immense amount of work that you have put in over the years.

Win ShepherdJean

Column · page 66

Arthur Penstone suffered a broken leg last Autumn and had to undergo a spell in hospital as a result. The plaster cast was off by December and Arthur is now getting around ok with the aid of a stick. Saturday afternoons are not the same without him pottering around fixing up the electrical bits and pieces but we look forward to seeing him back at Belgrave Hall soon.

Ken Miller also went into hospital for an operation last September. Luckily he was not out of action for long because he was seen cheering on the Belgrave runners from his garden while they were contesting the Hog's Back Road Race.

It's good to hear that Derek Jones has made a great recovery from his heart valve bypass operation last year and we look forward very much to seeing him back at Battersea Park this summer

Arthur PenstoneKen MillerDerek Jones

Advertisement · page 67

St. Mary's University College, Strawberry Hill

Thinking about further education? Why not consider a College of the University of Surrey:

St. Mary's University College, Strawberry Hill

St. Mary's is one of the country's leading higher education establishments with a long association with the University of Surrey. It is situated in the borough of Richmond Upon Thames and is just half an hour away from the centre of London. It is a single site campus with all facilities on site. This includes halls of residence, lecture rooms, all sporting facilities and (most importantly) the Student Union. Sporting facilities include weight room, indoor sportshall, dance studio, gym, running track and sports injuries clinic.

St. Mary's is an excellent place to study and being so close to London, it is also a great place from which to be able to train. Athletic tracks that are easy to get to are Hounslow, Kingsmeadow, Battersea Park and Crystal Palace and there are long runs available through nearby Bushy Park, Richmond Park and Wimbledon Common. Without even leaving campus you'll be able to use the weights room, take part in circuit training twice a week and run around the 'college mile'. So there's no excuse for not being able to carry on with your regular training even though you're at university.

Courses

Physical Education

Concentrating on teaching but the skills gained will be useful in other careers such as coaching, sport management and personnel work.

Sport Rehabilitation

Students acquire a variety of skills in sport rehabilitation and sport massage and the course enables students to work specifically in the area of sport injury and rehabilitation.

Sport Science

Excellent stepping stone to careers in sports related fields. Ample opportunity to develop personal skills and to take additional coaching awards and sport massage. Subjects covered include anatomy and physiology, psychology of sport, sociocultural issues, biomechanics with an emphasis on athletics and practical sessions in many sports, again with an emphasis on athletics. Much of the specialised track & field programme is supported by well known and highly respected coaches such as Bruce Longden, Conrad Milton, Judy Vernon and Mike Smith. St. Mary's Athletic Club members include Belgravians Darren Thompson, Nic Cooper and Hazel Mead.

The lecturers are very approachable and are always willing to help. Practical work is an important part of the programme which allows students to test theory against practice and makes the degree relevant to real sporting environments. In the second and third years, students are able to specialise in their chosen sports and have the opportunity to undertake a dissertation on any area of interest.

From an athlete's point of view

St. Mary's has a long tradition of sporting achievement and there are many clubs and societies that you can join. This includes a highly successful athletics club, that enters teams in cross country, road racing and track and field. In March 1999, our student athletic team will be travelling up to Glasgow for the British Universities Indoor Championships and in May the team will go to Bedford for the Outdoor Championships. During the winter students have competed in the London Colleges Cross Country League, the Hyde Park Relays and many other events. Last year four St. Mary's athletes were selected to represent British Students in the annual Loughborough v. A.A.A. v. Great Britain Under 20ís and we even had a representative in the Commonwealth Games in Kuala Lumpur.

Nic Cooper writes:

Training facilities for all athletic events are suitable for all standards and a number of internationals regularly use the impressively equipped weights rooms and physiology lab. Sports equipment is available for student use and covers all sports generously and includes alternative training aids such as video analysis and electronic equipment. On site there is a physiotherapy and injury rehabilitation clinic and massage rooms. Although these provisions are not exclusively for student use, reduced rates are arranged and students are willing to give massage for further practice. Staff are sympathetic to the needs of elite sports people studying at St. Mary's and consideration is given if international competition or training abroad is necessary. St. Mary's is an excellent place to study and live. The grounds are pleasant and relaxing and Twickenham is still only half an hour away from London and the other facilities across London that Belgrave athletes often use.

ST MARY'S UNIVERSITY COLLEGE Strawberry Hill

For further information, particularly about our Foundation Programme, or for a chat or a prospectus, contact:

Allan Staerk, M.Sc., Senior Lecturer, Department of Sport, Health and Exercise Science with responsibility for Track & Field Tel: 0181 240 4319 Fax: 0181 240 4255

or Hazel Mead Tel: [phone number withheld]
